# Implementation decisions of the migration to bundles

**Status:** record of the decisions made while implementing the [bundle architecture](../bundles.md). It settles the points that the concept documents leave open or contradictory. When a concept document and this record disagree, this record is what holds for the code; the documentation has been frozen since phase 0 of bundle isolation.

> below that cite them are history. The current behavior is in

> Changes view and its counter read the store of `git` directly

> `project`, `thread` and `thread_event`. The entries below that describe them
> as current (S-12, the refusal in `connect()` X-14, stages 4 and 5, the part of

> `storage.backend/v1` and `secrets.backend/v1`, and the startup check refuses,
> with or without a marker, a database in which the three legacy tables exist and one
> of them has a row.

## Principles

- **1:1 parity.** The migration preserves the current observable behavior, including quirks documented in the inventory (for example `POST /threads/:id/interrupt` ignores `promptId`, the Tools section of the settings does not reach the host, only the Thread selection persists in the renderer, the app has only a dark theme, `alwaysAvailable` filters neither tools nor skills, the `resume_exhausted` message in Portuguese). No new product functionality enters; bug fixes stay out of this migration.
- **Minimal API.** When the documents do not name a method needed to preserve a current functionality, the method enters with the smallest shape that covers that functionality and is marked here.
- **Combined stages.** The stages of the [migration](./migration.md) are review cuts. Stages 5, 6 and 7 are implemented together: `runtime` and `direct` are born on the generic types and streams of `storage/v1`, and the data migration converts the current tables. Stage 2 delivers the generic primitives of stage 3 directly in `postgres`.
- **Stage 11.** External bundle installation, signing and consent remain deferred. `notifications` and `transport` enter because they correspond to current functionality.

## Folder and package structure

| Decision                     | Value                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| ---------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Folders of the parts         | `host/`, `desktop/` (`desktopMain` environment), `client/`, `contracts/`, `skills/` inside `bundles/<id>/src` (source) and `dist` (artifact), following the manifest convention. The examples with `desktopMain/` are illustrative.                   |
| npm package name of a bundle | Same as the bundle ID (`core`, `files`, `git`, `chat`...). The library packages `packages/docker` and `packages/firecracker` are renamed `docker-sandbox` and `firecracker-sandbox` to free the names of the `docker` and `firecracker` bundles.      |
| `<bundle>/contracts`         | Subpath `./contracts` in the `exports` of the bundle's package; in the browser, import map entry `"<id>/contracts"` for the generation's artifact.                                                                                                    |
| `bundle/contracts`           | Browser-safe subpath of `packages/bundle`; no Node import reachable from it. It also contains the zod schemas of the basic contracts (windows, menus, ui, navigation, regions), so that replacement providers validate the same way.                  |
| Services runtime             | The services directory, the dispatcher and the propagation of `call` live in an internal browser-safe subpath of `packages/bundle` (`bundle/runtime`), used by the host loader, by `desktopMain` and by `packages/desktop`. Bundles do not import it. |
| New packages                 | `packages/storage` (protocol types and conformance kit) and, after consolidation, `packages/desktop` (client runtime of the loader, read helpers, shadcn components, tokens, Tailwind and theme).                                                     |
| Build of the parts           | `host/` with `tsc` as today; `client/` and `desktop/` with esbuild in ESM, leaving the shared packages and the `<id>/contracts` external. CSS of each client bundle generated with Tailwind v4 and served as `client/styles.css`.                     |

## Contracts and services

- **Environment and cardinality before the code.** The build generates, together with the JSON Schemas, one descriptor per bundle (`dist/contracts.json`) with each provided contract, its environment, cardinality and kind (service, hooks, event, command, settings, storage). The loader uses the descriptor before activation and checks it in `provide`/`register`.
- **`requires` per environment.** A `requires` entry constrains the part of an environment when some enabled bundle provides the contract in that environment. `client` parts are also satisfied by providers of `host` and `desktopMain` through the bridge. Entries with no reachable provider are a missing dependency.
- **Cardinality.** A fixed table in `bundle/contracts` for the basic ones (`many` only for `models.provider/v1`); for the others, the descriptor of the owning bundle. `use` of a `many` contract throws `ContractError` with code `many_contract_use`.
- **`whenAvailable`.** Callback `(api, provider: { bundleId, version }) => void | Cleanup`. What the callback registers is disposed of when that provider deactivates.
- **`use` outside `requires`.** Throws `ContractError` `undeclared_requirement`, except for `doric.*` contracts, contracts the bundle itself provides, and the core using basic contracts.
- **Scope.** Effective scope = the parent's `call.scope` merged with the scope of `use` (keys of `use` prevail); the dispatcher overwrites `windowKey` in the calls that leave a window.
- **`deadline` and `trace`.** `deadline` in epoch milliseconds, `0` means no deadline. `trace` is `{ traceparent: string }` in W3C format; each hop creates a child span with the same trace ID.
- **Errors.** `bundle/contracts` exports `ContractError` (`code`, `message`, `details?`). Values thrown without `code` become `internal`; validation failures become `invalid_input` and `invalid_output`.
- **Synchronous methods.** Local-only services (with `z.custom` inputs or functions) return the result of `execute` directly, synchronous or not; the dispatcher refuses to carry them over the bridge.
- **Hooks.** Kinds `observe`, `transform` and `decide`. `execute` awaits the `observe` handlers in sequence, each with the default deadline; an `observe` failure goes to diagnostics. Only the bundle that lists the hooks contract in `provides` (or the platform, for `doric.*`) may call `define`; `define` is idempotent per owner.
- **Order between unrelated bundles.** Topological order by dependency with a lexical tie-break by `id`; the same order holds for hook handlers.
- **Inputs of the app hooks.** `onActivateHook`/`onDeactivateHook`: `{ bundleId, version, generation, environment }`; `onHostReadyHook`, `onAppReadyHook`, `onAppActivateHook`, `onBeforeQuitHook`: `{}`; `onGenerationReadyHook`: `{ generation }`; `onConnectionChangedHook`: `{ connected }`. All `.strict()`.
- **`log/v1`.** `debug | info | warn | error(message, fields?)`, without throwing and without waiting for a response.
- **`provide` followed by `use` in the same `activate`.** `provide` registers synchronously; the platform calls `define(AppHooksV1)` after the `activate` of the `hooks/v1` provider ends.
- **Commands.** Commands are versioned contracts and go in `provides`. Projection block types do not.
- **S-1 Refinements of stages 2 and 3.** Before any published baseline, stages 2 and 3 refine, without a semver event, the basic shapes of `storage`, `secrets`, `settings`, `events` and of the catalog (`CatalogSnapshot.storage`, `HostPlatform.storageIdentity()`) and add the codes `revision_conflict`, `sequence_conflict`, `unique_conflict`, `limit_exceeded`, `unknown_type`, `invalid_index_value`, `invalid_stream`, `unknown_settings`, `settings_missing`, `settings_conflict`, `unknown_event`, `invalid_scope` and `credential_*`. Core and `postgres` use these exact schemas (D-12).
- **S-6 Rules of the credential fields.** `credentialFields`, `credentialFieldsSatisfied`, `credentialFieldsMessage` and the field types live in `bundle/contracts`; the kernel (route schemas) and the core (validation) import from there, so the rule remains a single one.
- **S-7 `secrets/v1.view()`.** Local, synchronous view of the provider's cache (`list`, `redactions`, `addRedaction`), which never crosses the bridge. `secrets/v1.redactions(call)` (minimal API, serializable) returns the same redaction list to whoever is outside the process (cross-review X-4).
- **S-8 References instead of the FK.** `secrets/v1.registerReferences(fonte)` (minimal API, local): `delete` answers `referenced` while a registered source names the credential, and this is checked first. `settings/v1.set` and the writes of `secrets/v1` go through a single write queue of the core, so a configuration never names a credential removed by a concurrent delete. The in-process queue replaces the Serializable transaction of the configuration; the existing assumption of one host process per database holds, on which the credential cache already depended.
- **S-10 `events/v1`.** Writes to `runtime.thread.events/<threadId>` (`threadEventsStream`) the current envelope, `threadEventEnvelopeSchema` (`projectId`, `threadId`, `promptId`, `type`, `event`); `sequence` and `createdAt` are those of the protocol entry, and this is the only format of that stream for `runtime` too (X-5). The body carries `type`, redaction runs before writing, the call's scope must name project, Thread and prompt (`invalid_scope`), and only the owner of the registered event appends (`not_owner`). No published bundle registers an event until stage 7 (test E-6), because a direct append bypasses the `runtime` aggregate.
- **S-12 Provisional `postgres.database/v1`.** `connect()` and `client()` (local) and the subpath `postgres/prisma` (generated client and `createDatabase`) exist only for the kernel, whose Project, Thread, event and usage tables stay in the current SQL until stage 7.

## Composition and boot

- **Composition file.** The host reads the composition from `DORIC_COMPOSITION` (path of a JSON file) and, without the variable, from the default composition shipped with the host (`agents/doric/composition.json`). Format: `{ "core": "core", "enabled": [...], "providers": { ... } }`, validated by zod in `packages/bundle`. The installed bundles are read from `DORIC_BUNDLES_PATH` (default: the host's `dist/bundles` directory).
- **Foundation per environment.** A bundle chosen in `providers` is foundation only in the environments in which it provides the chosen contract; in the others, it activates with the rest. Foundation bundles that replace a facade of the core (`events/v1`, `settings/v1`, `models/v1`) may require `storage.backend/v1` and `secrets.backend/v1`, which activate earlier inside the foundation.
- **Generations.** A monotonic counter per published catalog. Kernel bundles report the generation in which the kernel activated them.
- **`hostId` and `storageGeneration`.** The `postgres` backend keeps a reserved metadata record with the two values; the catalog publishes them (S-5).
- **S-15 Boot order.** `bootstrap → sandbox_pool → bundle_load → database_client → credential_activation → configuration_activation → workspace_reconciliation → network_binding → ready`: only `database_client` moves, to after `bundle_load`. No `activate` of the foundation or of the core does I/O (client, cipher and `declareType` are lazy); the kernel forces each one in the stage that already failed because of it: in `database_client` only a missing `DORIC_DATABASE_URL` (the `connect()` of the `pg` adapter opens no connection, like the `$connect()` before); the storage identity is read right after the credentials load, in `credential_activation`, where an unreachable or unmigrated database already failed, and the configuration read in `configuration_activation`. Stage names, log message texts and the fatal path do not change; only the order of the startup lines follows that of the stages. `Bundles loaded` now counts 5 bundles (`postgres` enters; the built-in `host-adapter` and `host-config` do not count). On shutdown, the `$disconnect` happens in the deactivation of `postgres`, inside `platform.stop()`.

## Storage and data

- **`storage.backend/v1`.** Generic primitives: versioned documents by type and key with a revision, declared indexes (equality, `null` included, and ordering), streams with sequence and gaps, an atomic declarative `commit` (puts, deletes, appends, expected-revision check), a commit feed with a cursor, blobs, stream truncation and write health/admission. `packages/storage` defines the types and the conformance kit that any backend must pass.
- **Current data.** An offline, idempotent migration converts Project, Thread, events, queue, executor state, usage, configuration and providers to types and streams of the owning bundles, with a verification read before the switch. Credentials stay in the AES-256-GCM envelope under `DORIC_CREDENTIAL_KEY` and are read without rewriting the secret.
- **Events.** Written events are never rewritten; stage 7 copies the events to the new stream with the same type and payload. The type `prompt.finished` is now also read as `runtime.prompt-finished/v1`.
- **`settings/v1`.** `register(definição)`, `get(definição)`, `entry(definição)` (S-9), `set(definição, valor, { expectedRevision? })` and `subscribe`. Each definition is a space with `{ value, revision, version, updatedAt, generation }`. A global configuration generation counter preserves the provenance that Projects store today.
- **`secrets/v1`.** `resolve(ref)`, `list`, `describe(ref)` (never returns the secret), `create`, `update` (absent keeps, `''` clears, a value sets), `delete` (refused when referenced), `resolveByKind(kind, { exclude? })` and `redactions()`, plus the local `registerReferences` (S-8) and `view` (S-7). Redaction in the logs as today.
- **`usage`.** Stays in the `runtime` aggregate as long as there is no transactional participation of another bundle; the `usage` bundle reads the projection and provides the UI.
- **Disk pressure.** `postgres` reports measurements and admission (S-4); the caller keeps the current policy (10% threshold, a check every 3 s, mapping of `53100` and `ENOSPC`): the kernel's runner in stages 2 to 4 and `runtime` from stage 5 on.
- **S-2 `commit.dropStreams`.** Minimal API: removes whole streams, for the parity of Thread and Project deletion from stage 5 on. Order of application in an atomic batch, checked by the kit: expect → delete → put → dropStreams → truncate → append. The result stays `{ position }`; a writer with `expect` derives the new revision as expected + 1 (a creation with `revision` 0 gets 1; X-2).
- **S-3 Entries and truncation.** The protocol owns the `createdAt` of each stream entry (ISO-8601 in ms, UTC, set by the backend at commit) and the truncation marks `{ afterSequence, atSequence }`, which `read` returns to the affected readers (`afterSequence < pedido <= atSequence`). `lastSequence` never decreases. Each feed commit separates `streams` (appends), `truncated` and `dropped`.
- **S-4 Health.** `health` reports measurements: `{ status: 'ok' | 'unavailable', writable, reason?, disk?: { blocks, availableBlocks } }`. `'pressure'` leaves the enum, and a disk that cannot be measured is `unavailable` without `disk`. `storage/v1.health` enters (minimal API). The health of `postgres` measures `DORIC_STORAGE_PATH` without a round trip to the database. There is no `storage_full` code: database errors propagate unchanged, so `53100` remains recognizable. The facade has no `capabilities()`: it refuses with `limit_exceeded` before sending, and the default `maxBatch` is 10000, so that the atomic deletion of a large Project fits in one batch (X-3).
- **S-5 Identity.** `storage.backend/v1.identity()` creates on the first call the reserved record with `hostId` (random UUID) and `storageGeneration` 1 and returns the same values afterwards, in any instance. `HostPlatform.storageIdentity()` memoizes the promise (a rejection is not memoized) and puts the identity in `catalog().storage`; the kernel resolves it in `database_client`. The bundle generation counter stays per process; persisting it is left to the stage that needs it (cross-review G-6 hands it to the design of stage 8).
- **S-9 Settings spaces.** `settings/v1.entry` (minimal API), because `GET`/`PUT /config` need the revision and the time. `SettingsEntry` gains `updatedAt` and `generation`. Each space is a `core.settings/v1` document with the space's ID, and the global counter is the `global` document of `core.settings-generation/v1`; each `set` increments the space's revision and the counter. Subscribers run synchronously after the commit, before the write queue releases. Codes: `unknown_settings`, `settings_missing`, `settings_conflict` and `invalid_input`.
- **S-11 Index keys.** A byte-orderable encoding: `null` < `false` < `true` < number < string; numbers in numeric order, strings by UTF-8 bytes; ties broken by the document ID, also in UTF-8 bytes (like `COLLATE "C"`).
- **S-13 Configuration in `settings/v1`.** The configuration moves to the space `host-config.configuration/v1`, of the provisional built-in bundle `host-config` (kernel). The SQL migration `20261009000000_generic_storage` converts `doric_configuration`, `provider_configuration` and `model_configuration` without loss, keeping `revision` and `updated_at` (ms, UTC `Z`) and starting the global counter at the revision; it verifies the conversion before removing these tables and the enum `ModelRole`. A provider kind outside `openai`, `openrouter` and `codex` stops the migration (`RAISE`), without discarding or converting anything. The `generation` UUID of each replacement, which nobody read, goes away; Projects keep storing the revision. Without the FK, the `credential` table stands alone: the references are guaranteed by S-8 and by the refinement of `host-config`. The migration is not reversible (pre-1.0 policy): the operator takes a backup before `prisma migrate deploy`. The split of the space among the owners is left to stage 10 (X-6).
- **S-14 Feed positions.** Allocated under a transaction advisory lock, taken last in the commit, so the feed has no gaps and follows commit order. `NOTIFY` waits for a publisher (stage 5 onward).
- **S-16 Prisma in `postgres`.** The schema, the migrations (the 17 earlier ones unchanged plus `generic_storage`) and the generated client live in `bundles/foundation/postgres`. `agents/doric/prisma.config.ts` remains as a compatible entry point (Compose, image, `nx run doric:migrate`). What Prisma does not express (partial unique index, `COLLATE "C"`) belongs to the migration's SQL, like the trigger and the CHECK before; the flow is only `migrate deploy`.

## Execution and domain

- **Sandbox access from the tools.** `runtime.lease/v1` exposes the operations of the sandbox acquired for `call.scope.projectId` (exec, files, Git, diff, metrics), answering with the current lease states without acquiring. `files/v1`, `git`, `terminal/v1` and `resources` use this contract.
- **Methods of `runtime.*`, `terminal/v1` and `files/v1`.** Derived one by one from the current controls of `packages/host` and from the current Project, Thread and terminal routes.
- **Tool configuration.** Goes to the `defineSettings` of the owning bundle and is read with `services.use(SettingsV1).get(...)` inside `execute`.
- **Skills.** The loader discovers `skills/*/SKILL.md` and registers through `skills/v1` on behalf of the bundle; `skills/v1.register(skill)` exists for registration from code; `skills/v1.list()` preserves the order of the bundles and the resolution of `allowed-tools` per bundle; `direct` reads skills and tools through `skills/v1` and `tools/v1`.
- **`models.provider/v1`.** `{ kind(): KindDescriptor; listModels(config, call); create(config, secrets, call): LlmProvider }`, mirroring `providerKinds` and `createProviderForKind` of `packages/llms`. `models/v1.resolve({ providerId, model, effort }, call)`.
- **Transport.** The `transport` bundle mounts the current REST routes and Socket.IO namespaces on the semantic operations of the bundles, preserving paths, shapes, error codes and headers (including the global `Cache-Control: no-store`). In addition, the host exposes a technical bridge endpoint (`/doric/bridge`, Socket.IO) with the messages `call`, `result`, `cancel`, `subscribe`, `event` and `unsubscribe`, used by the desktop for calls to host services, the catalog and the download of artifacts by hash.
- **Processes.** On the host, the kernel (`agents/doric`) runs the technical transport, the catalog, the foundation and the core; the other bundles run in one child process per generation (`child_process.fork`) with the RPC envelope. On the desktop, all `desktopMain` parts of a generation (foundation, core and the others) run in the `utilityProcess` of that generation.

## Desktop and UI

- **Preload.** A single namespace `window.doric.desktop`: `describeWindow()`, `ready()`, `reportFailure(info)`, `onCloseRequested(cb)` with a response channel, `onChromeChanged(cb)`, `catalog()`, `call(envelope)`, `cancel(id)` and stream subscription. The current semantic channels go away when the UI in bundles takes over.
- **Import map.** React, ReactDOM, Lexical and `@lexical/*`, Base UI (`@base-ui/react` and its subpaths, since 2026-10-17), `desktop/ui`, `zod`, `bundle/contracts` and the generation's `<id>/contracts`. The protocol handler renders the shell per request with the generation's import map and computes the CSP hash in the same response.
- **Platform versions.** `PLATFORM_VERSION` (`packages/bundle/src/runtime/version.ts`, `1.1.0`) is the version against which the catalog checks `engines.doric` and which every `doric.*` provider carries; it goes up one minor per phase that adds to the platform (1.1.0: `desktop/ui/identity` and `desktop/ui/reader-avatar`). `PLATFORM_ASSETS_VERSION` (`1.0.0`), next to it, is the version segment of the platform's folders and URLs (`desktop@<v>`, `bundle@<v>`, `splash@<v>`, `shell@<v>`) and does not follow a platform minor (S35 of `docs/designs/ui-redesign.md`).
- **Dev.** In development the windows also open through `doric://app`; the handler forwards the shell and the assets to the development server.
- **Scheme privileges.** `standard`, `secure`, `supportFetchAPI`, `corsEnabled` and `codeCache`, confirmed in the first test of stage 8.
- **Windows.** `open({ key, path })` always honors `path`. The main process keeps the last path and the bounds of each key. In `onAppReadyHook`, the core reopens `workspace` at the last stored path (or `/`), which preserves the Thread selection across restarts; the old selection in `localStorage` (`doric.selection`) is converted to a path once.
- **Generations on the desktop.** On a switch, the new `utilityProcess` starts, defines `AppHooksV1` and fires `onGenerationReadyHook` (not `onAppReadyHook`); open windows stay in the generation they were born in and reload to switch.
- **Commands.** The client's `commands/v1` looks first in the window's registry and then forwards to `desktopMain` through the bridge. The main process resolves menu and shortcut in the registry of `desktopMain` and then in the focused window. Commands bound to a menu or shortcut accept empty input.
- **Shortcuts.** Roles of the Edit menu keep native accelerators; the other shortcuts are handled by a single listener in the shell, which receives from the main process the effective table filtered by `when`. Since the app uses Electron's default menu today, the core's skeleton reproduces that default menu and the current shortcuts.
- **Lexical.** `chat` freezes its own node registry when the window's first editor is created.
- **Renderers.** Strict `registerRenderer({ blockType, elementTag })`; block actions are command IDs, dispatched by `chat` through a single intent event `{ command, input }`.
- **Notifications.** Platform capability `doric.notifications/v1` in `desktopMain` (`show({ title, body })`, with the same report of a system refusal that exists today); the `notifications` bundle decides when to notify from `runtime.prompt-finished/v1`.
- **Local history.** The `history` bundle provides in `desktopMain` `history.snapshots/v1` (`read(threadId)`, `write`), writing to `<hostId>/<storageGeneration>/<threadId>` inside the directory of `doric.cache/v1`; `chat` uses it through `whenAvailable`.
- **Routes.** Matching by `pathname` only; trailing slash normalized, except on `/`; static segments before parameters; patterns of the same specificity that match the same paths are a conflict.
- **React elements.** Idempotent `dispose()`; the helper `defineReactElement` lives in `desktop/ui`, not in `desktop/client`.
- **Layout tabs.** Tab state indexed by the `threadId` of the active route.
- **Settings sections.** The configuration and credential contracts are host services (`settings/v1`, `secrets/v1`, `models/v1`) reached through the bridge.

## Validation

- Every phase passes `npx nx run-many -t typecheck test build`, `npm run format:check` and `npm run lint`.
- Parity is verified by an end-to-end suite that runs the same API scenarios (REST and Socket.IO) and UI scenarios (Electron over CDP) against the baseline and against the new implementation, with a fake model server compatible with OpenAI Responses.
- The host's Docker image (`agents/doric/.Dockerfile`, targets `agent-runtime`, `docker-runtime` and `runtime`) compiles all bundles, including the `client` and `desktop` artifacts that the desktop downloads during the splash.

## Stage 1 (Contract)

Decisions made while implementing stage 1. The identifiers **D-n** are cited in the code and in the tests.

### Contracts and services runtime

- **D-1 Order of the skills.** The loader discovers `skills/*/SKILL.md` in lexical folder order. The folders of `files` carry two-digit prefixes that reproduce the order of the old manifest, so the system prompt is identical byte for byte.
- **D-2 Brands and errors across instances.** Definitions, `Call` and the registration protocol use `Symbol.for`; errors are recognized by `name`/`code`, never by `instanceof`; the runtime's maps use the contract ID as the key.
- **D-3 Call argument at the end.** A `Call` at the end is always the call argument. Options `{ signal, deadline }` count as the call argument only beyond the length of the tuple or when the tuple item at that position refuses them; so `log.info('x', { deadline: 5 })` keeps `deadline` as a field.
- **D-4 Registrations.** Every registration API returns `Disposable` (`{ dispose() }`), `navigation.subscribe` included.
- **D-7 Original output.** Inside the process the output is validated and the original value is returned, never zod's copy; output schemas are descriptive.
- **D-8 Original error.** Inside the process the value thrown by `execute` propagates unchanged; `toContractError` only where a code is required.
- **D-10 Registration protocol.** `bindRegistrant` binds a registrable argument (tool, command) to the services of the calling bundle before validation.
- **D-11 Synchronous `invalid_input`.** Argument validation throws synchronously in the process; the tool wrappers are `async`, so the caller sees a rejection.
- **D-12 Providers of basic contracts.** `provide` checks the method names and the JSON Schema of each method against the platform's specification (`contract_mismatch`).
- **D-13 `whenAvailable`.** Each callback runs in a child scope bound to the life of that provider.
- **D-14 Platform caller.** `{ bundleId: 'doric', version: PLATFORM_VERSION, generation }`.
- **D-16 Facade of `hooks/v1`.** The provider implements flat methods (`define`, `register`, `whenDefined`, `execute`); the runtime builds the consumer API.
- **D-17 Inputs of `WindowHooksV1`.** Strict `{}`.
- **D-18 `doric.log/v1`.** New platform contract (minimal API), `write(record)`, through which a `log/v1` provider reaches the host's log files.
- **D-29 Browser safety.** Besides the import-graph test, `tsconfig.browser.json` (typecheck only, `lib` es2022+dom, `types: []`) compiles `bundle/contracts` and `bundle/runtime`.
- **D-30 Pure parts in the runtime.** The manifest, composition and descriptor schemas, the per-environment resolver `resolveEnvironment` and the generic activation loop live in `bundle/runtime`; `loader/` keeps only file reading, discovery, skills and the Node `startHost`.
- **D-31 `skills/v1.list`.** Asynchronous (non-local method).
- **D-32 Hooks `define`.** The provider's `define` returns a `Disposable` registered in the owner's scope; disposing of it drops the registrations made inside `whenDefined` callbacks. The facade returns one handle per contract.
- **D-33 No signal race.** The in-process dispatcher neither checks nor races against `call.signal`/the deadline; cancellation reaches the providers only through `call.signal`.
- **D-34 JSON Schema per direction.** `io: 'input'` for inputs and `io: 'output'` for outputs.
- **Reserved platform.** `defineService` refuses `doric.*` IDs, like `defineHooks`; the platform uses an internal variant.
- **Read-only outputs.** `defineMethod` accepts readonly forms of the output type.
- **Call to a nonexistent method.** A method the provider does not have answers `unavailable`.
- **Typed loose schemas.** The record schemas of `runtime/contracts` and `terminal/contracts` are `z.looseObject` annotated with the matching interface (`z.ZodType<T, T>`), so that the provider returns the type it already has.

### Manifest, composition, catalog and activation

- **D-19 Tools v0.** A v0 manifest tool must default-export a current `tool` definition; an old factory puts the bundle in quarantine (`invalid_part`).
- **D-20 Backends in the composition.** The rule of `storage.backend/v1`/`secrets.backend/v1` always in `providers` starts in stage 2.
- **D-21 Foundation in quarantine.** A chosen foundation provider in quarantine takes the host to recovery, without falling back to the core. A chosen provider that is not a foundation one, in quarantine, leaves the contract without an active provider, and its dependents get `dependency_quarantined`.
- **D-22 Environment from the build.** The environment and the cardinality of each contract in the descriptor come from a dry-run activation in the build, not from a declaration.
- **D-35 Manifest.** The `id` cannot be `doric`; converted v0 manifests get an empty descriptor. A bundle whose manifest cannot be read has no known ID and is always quarantined (`invalid_manifest`).
- **Missing root.** An unreadable bundles root becomes a composition problem and recovery (core missing), not an exception.
- **Counters.** The generation is a per-process counter; the contract providers in the catalog snapshot also carry `bundleVersion`.
- **Core exemption in the build.** The build recorder does not exempt the core from declaring basic contracts (in the build there is no composition); in stage 1 the core uses only contracts it provides and `doric.*`.

### Core, bundles and adapter

- **D-5 `runtime.lease/v1`.** Enters in stage 1 in the adapter with `describe`, `exec`, `readFile` and `writeFile`; `exec` does not pass a cancellation signal on to the sandbox and does not return bytes.
- **D-6 Optional terminal.** `files` consumes `terminal/v1` through `whenAvailable` (outside `requires`) and keeps the fallback of the `terminal` tool to a simple command in the sandbox, refusing `background` and `pty` with `This host does not support live terminals.`. The adapter answers `unavailable` for a prompt whose `Host` has no terminals, which leads to the same fallback.
- **D-9 Tool configuration.** The values no longer reach `execute`; the metadata `settings`, `requireToolConfigs`, `resolveToolConfigs` (still called on each prompt, which keeps refusing unsatisfied settings) and `GET /tools` keep their behavior until `settings/v1`.
- **D-15 Recovery on the host.** In stage 1 the recovery mode ends in the existing fatal startup path (`Doric failed to start`, stage `bundle_load`) after logging `Bundle recovery mode` with the state and the reasons of each bundle.
- **D-23 Core contracts on the desktop.** `core.hooks/v1` and `core.open-settings/v1` enter the core's `provides` in stage 8; `core/contracts` already publishes the references.
- **D-24 Hook deadline.** 10 000 ms per handler in the core; `doric.window.hooks/v1` follows its own rules (`onCloseHook` without a deadline, `onFlushHook` with the call's deadline).
- **D-25 Names.** Nx projects `bundle-<id>`; npm packages `<id>`.
- **D-26 Contract-only packages.** `bundles/runtime/runtime` and `bundles/features/terminal` have existed since stage 1 only as `runtime/contracts` and `terminal/contracts`, without a manifest and without staging.
- **D-27 Built-in adapter.** The built-in bundle `host-adapter` (always enabled by the host, outside the composition) provides `runtime.threads/v1`, `runtime.workspace/v1`, `runtime.lease/v1` and `terminal/v1` on top of the `Host` and the sandbox of each prompt, bound to the prompt's scope during execution (`attach`/`release`, with the release guarded by the identity of the binding). It goes away when the extracted bundles take over these contracts.
- **D-28 Diagnostics without a route.** No HTTP diagnostics route in stage 1; diagnostics live in `doric.diagnostics/v1`, `HostPlatform.diagnostics()` and in the `Bundle quarantined` log lines.
- **Required executor.** The workspace service receives the Direct executor from the composition root (`createDirectExecutor({ call, attach })`); there is no default executor any more.
- **Paths.** `DORIC_BUNDLES_PATH` (default: `dist/bundles` next to the compiled entrypoint) and `DORIC_COMPOSITION` (default: `agents/doric/composition.json`).
- **Accepted observable changes.** `Bundles loaded` counts 4 bundles instead of 3 (the new core); a non-core bundle that fails is quarantined instead of preventing startup; the stack frames of errors persisted in `tool.failed`/`agent.failed` change path, keeping `name`, `message` and `cause`.

### Open points

- Stage 4: invocation of tools and hook handlers across processes (handler references in the envelope); the envelope's error must keep `name`, `message` and the `cause` chain for the parity of the `tool.failed`/`agent.failed` events; local-only methods (`tools/v1.register/list`, `hooks/v1.register`) need handles on the envelope side; cleanup in the provider by `caller.generation` when a generation leaves.
- Stage 2: tool settings values through `settings/v1`; output of `events/v1.append`; the rule of the backends in `providers`; persisted generation counter and `hostId`.
- Stages 8/9: descriptor recorder with a DOM for `client` parts; registrations that return `Promise<void>` over the bridge (`menus/v1`) disposed of on the provider side in `onDeactivateHook`; promote the minimal test core to `bundle/testing`.
- Log file rotation does not enter in stage 1; `doric.log/v1` writes to the existing pino destinations.
- Access by scope (HC-007): any bundle that requires `runtime.lease/v1`, `runtime.threads/v1` or `terminal/v1` and builds a scope with the IDs of a running prompt reaches that prompt's controls. All stage 1 bundles are first-party and external bundles remain deferred; stage 5 must keep these shapes (minor extension) or record a major change.

## Stages 2 and 3 (Storage and generic protocol)

Decisions made while implementing stages 2 and 3 together. The design decisions **S-1** to **S-16** are in the general sections above (Contracts and services, Composition and boot, Storage and data); the subsections below record the details of each package.

### Packages

- **Name `postgres`.** The bundle's npm package is `postgres` (Nx `bundle-postgres`), like the other bundles. The `postgres@3.4.7` dependency of `prisma` stays nested in `node_modules/prisma/node_modules/postgres`, and `prisma --version` works; there is no name exception. The protocol package is `storage` (Nx `storage`).

### Contracts (`packages/bundle`)

- **Exported credential kinds.** Besides the field rules (S-6), `bundle/contracts` exports `credentialKinds`, from which `credentialKindSchema` comes, so that the kernel derives the routes' `z.enum` from the same list.
- **Documented update rule.** The comment of `credentialUpdateSchema` now states the real rule of the current service: absent or `null` keeps the field; any string, `''` included, writes. The shape does not change.
- **`storageIdentity()` without a backend.** With no active provider of `storage.backend/v1`, `HostPlatform.storageIdentity()` answers `undefined` without memoizing. The resolved identity appears in `catalog().storage` and in `doric.catalog/v1.get()`.
- **Schema names.** The backend's declaration is exported as `storageDeclarationSchema`/`StorageDeclaration`; `hostId` uses `z.uuid()` (the zod 4 equivalent of `z.string().uuid()`). `feedRequestSchema.after` does not change.

### Protocol (`packages/storage`)

- **Helpers beyond the design.** The root of `storage` also exports `decodeIndexKey` (inverse of the encoding), `compareBytes`, `compareIds` (tie-break by ID in UTF-8 bytes, like `COLLATE "C"`), `defaultStorageCapabilities` and `checkBatchLimits`, so that memory, `postgres` and the core's facade apply the same limits.
- **Indexable values.** A non-finite number, a string with a lone surrogate, an object or an array is `invalid_index_value`; `-0` and `0` have the same key. A range with `gt` and `gte` (or `lt` and `lte`) together is `invalid_input`.
- **`parseStreamId`.** `family` is the qualified name before the slash, with the owner (`runtime.thread.events`); `owner` is the first segment. The backend accepts any stream name; validating the format is the facade's role.
- **Batch and limits.** An empty batch is one with `batchSize` 0 (`invalid_input`); a batch with only `expect` is valid and takes a position in the feed. The limits are inclusive, and `maxAppendBytes` applies to each appended event. Uniqueness is checked at each `put`, like a non-deferred unique index of PostgreSQL.
- **Truncation mark.** `atSequence` is the `lastSequence` at the end of the batch, counting the appends of the same batch, so that a reader that already had the removed tail always sees the mark.
- **Query and feed.** Querying an undeclared index (or an undeclared type), equality with more fields than the index, or a range without a following field is `invalid_input`. `documents` in the feed are the keys of `delete` and `put`, without repetition.
- **Blobs.** `putBlob` requires standard base64 (otherwise `invalid_input`); `getBlob` of a malformed hash answers `undefined`.
- **In-memory backends.** The in-memory `secrets.backend/v1` sorts `list` by name in code units (like `COLLATE "C"`), keeps the stored `kind` on an update and fills in `createdAt`/`updatedAt`. `memoryFoundation` also accepts `storage` (limits and `health` of the storage backend).
- **Conformance kit.** `storage/conformance` exports `conformanceCases` (49 cases, IDs D1–D7, C1–C4, R1–R3, Q1–Q10, S1–S6, P1–P2, F1–F4, X1–X5, B1–B3, L1–L3, H1–H2), `runConformanceCase(harness, caso)` and `runStorageConformance(harness, { skip, timeout })`, which registers one `node:test` suite per group (a limit of 120 s per case). Each case runs after `reset()`, and the instances it opens and neither closes nor crashes are closed at the end. The harness is generic in the instance type.
- **Backend-independent cases.** The kit does not assume that feed positions start at 1 or that they are contiguous (storage.md says only "monotonic"): it requires strictly increasing positions, each successful commit once and in order, and that a reader following concurrent commits misses none. The order of the keys inside a feed commit is not checked. The contiguity of `postgres` (S-14) stays in the bundle's own test.
- **Grouped design items.** The invalid queries go into Q1, Q2 and Q5; concurrent creations with `revision` 0 into C1; concurrent claims of a unique key into C4; `invalid_index_value` in the middle of the batch into X2; ISO `createdAt` into S1. The kit does not fix whether a unique key admits several documents without the field: today memory and `postgres` treat `null` as a value, and a unique index accepts only one document with the field absent.
- **Kit self-test.** `tests/kit-self.test.ts` wraps the in-memory backend in three broken variants (without atomicity, with a sequence reused after truncation, with a gap in the feed) and requires X1–X4, S3–S4 and F1–F4 to fail; any error counts as a failure. A wrapper that only forwards the calls passes the same cases.

### `postgres` backend (`bundles/foundation/postgres`)

- **Locking.** Instead of `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`, each `commit` takes at once, in a global order (by the `hashtextextended` key), transaction advisory locks: exclusive per document of `expect`/`put`/`delete` and per written stream, shared per type of `put`/`delete`; `declareType` takes the type's exclusive lock. That way a creation with `revision` 0, an absence check without `put` and a removed stream are serialized too, without a wait cycle. The position lock (S-14) comes last.
- **Puts one by one.** The `put`s apply in order, each with its revision and its index entries; uniqueness is checked at each `put`, with one `INSERT` per unique index, so that the `23505` on `storage_index_entry_unique_key` becomes `unique_conflict` with the index name. The other database errors propagate unchanged (`53100` remains recognizable).
- **Declarations without a cache.** `commit` and `query` read `storage_type` on every call, so a declaration made by another process holds at once.
- **Feed.** `storage_commit` gains the columns `truncated` and `dropped` (default `'[]'`) instead of keeping these names inside `streams`. A freshly migrated database starts at position 1, the commit of the configuration conversion.
- **Time.** One `clock_timestamp()` per commit, truncated to milliseconds and read after the locks, holds for `updated_at` and for the `createdAt` of the entries, in the format of `toISOString()`.
- **JSON.** Values are stored as JSONB: the order of the keys is JSONB's, and `\u0000` in values, IDs or stream names is refused by PostgreSQL (error propagated), unlike the in-memory backend; the facade applies `storable` first.
- **Transactions.** Prisma's interactive transactions use a `maxWait` of 30 s and a `timeout` of 120 s, because a batch can have up to `maxBatch` items and wait for locks.
- **Client and environment.** `client()` creates the client on first use from `DORIC_DATABASE_URL` (a failure is not memoized); `connect()` and the storage methods reject, without throwing synchronously, when the client cannot be created. The deactivation cleanup disconnects only the client created from the URL; an injected client (tests) stays with whoever created it. `createDatabase` is the one from before, so the per-model queries use the `public` schema.
- **Secrets.** The cipher is created on the first call of `list`, `get` or `put` (a malformed key refuses every call with the same message, before the database); a decryption failure is `credential_unreadable` with the cipher's message; `list`/`get` return `createdAt`/`updatedAt`. IDs that are not UUIDs reach Prisma as in the previous store.
- **Configuration migration.** The providers of the migrated document are in `ORDER BY id` in the database's collation; the old loading sorted by `localeCompare` on read, and the new reader must keep sorting. `jsonb_typeof(models) = 'array'` reproduces `Array.isArray`; the check also requires the document of the global counter. FK names and `ON UPDATE CASCADE` follow Prisma, and `prisma migrate diff` reports no difference.
- **Generated client.** `bundles/foundation/postgres/src/generated/prisma` stays out of git (`src/generated/.gitignore`), like the kernel's client before.
- **Conformance kit.** `tests/conformance.test.ts` runs `runStorageConformance` with the harness of `tests/support/harness.ts` on one migrated schema per file; closing an instance also disconnects its client, so as not to exhaust the connections. `tests/storage-*.test.ts` remain as direct evidence on PostgreSQL.

### Core facades (`bundles/foundation/core`)

- **Core version.** The core moves to `1.1.0`: the new contracts in `provides` are a minor change against the baseline published in stage 1, and the baseline is regenerated.
- **Dependency on `storage`.** The core's host part imports `parseStreamId` and `checkBatchLimits` from `storage`, to apply the same stream rules and limits as the backends. The package goes into the core's dependencies and must be in the staging and in the host image.
- **Registrations.** `storage/v1.defineType` repeated by the same owner with the same JSON Schema and the same indexes returns another handle, and the type goes away when the last one is disposed of. `settings/v1.register` and `events/v1.register` refuse the second definition of the same ID with `registration_conflict`.
- **Platform.** `doric` passes the owner checks and reads without validation documents of types that nobody defines. Every write requires the defined type, for `doric` too, because the `put` is validated by the type's schema.
- **Index fields.** A dotted field must exist in the zod schema, going through `optional`, `nullable`, `default`, `lazy`, `pipe` (by the output), union, intersection, `record` and numeric positions of `array`/`tuple`; `unknown`, `any` and `custom` accept any path.
- **Streams.** Only the owner of the stream (first segment) or `doric` reads. The active provider of `events/v1` only gains the write.
- **Never-written space.** `entry` answers with the schema's defaults with `revision` 0, `generation` 0, `updatedAt` at the epoch (`1970-01-01T00:00:00.000Z`) and the version from `provides`.
- **Registered definition.** `get`, `entry` and `set` use the schema of the definition registered with that ID, not that of the received object. A stored value that the schema refuses on read is `invalid_output`. Read, write and subscription do not check the owner, because `settings/v1` does not define `not_owner`.
- **Subscribers.** A subscriber's failure goes to `doric.diagnostics/v1` as `settings_listener_failed`, in the bundle that subscribed.
- **Redaction in `events/v1`.** The append uses `secrets/v1.redactions(call)`, which loads the credentials if needed, instead of `view()`: an event is never written without redaction, and a load failure fails the append. The body goes through `eventJson` and then through the `storable` of each string, like the kernel's `json()`; property names are redacted and, as in the kernel, do not go through `storable`.
- **Redaction in the log.** Strings of plain records, arrays, `Error` (with `name`, `message`, `stack` and `cause`) and instances with enumerable properties are redacted in a plain copy; instances without an enumerable property (`Date`, `Map`) stay as they are, because the destination does not write their content. `addRedaction('')` is ignored in `view()` too.

- **Storage types in `core/contracts`.** Besides `CoreHooksV1` and `OpenSettingsCommandV1`, `core/contracts` exports the core's own storage types, `core.settings/v1` and `core.settings-generation/v1` (with `settingsGenerationId`), so that the data migrations and their tests use the same definitions. This widens the rule "`core/contracts` exports only ..." of the concept documents.
- **Owner of the definitions.** `settings/v1.register` and `events/v1.register` check the namespace, the presence of the ID in `provides` and the kind of the received object (`defineSettings`/`defineEvent`), but not the kind of the declared descriptor: the catalog does not yet carry the kind of each contract from `dist/contracts.json`. The check enters when the loader publishes that kind in the catalog (the stage of external installation, when descriptors stop being trustworthy by construction).

### Kernel `agents/doric` (stages 2 to 4)

- **`host-config` per instance.** `createHostConfig()` returns the bundle, the space definition and `track`; the refinement that requires stored credentials reads the `view()` of that host, without module state, so that several hosts in the same process (tests) do not mix.
- **Refinement on read too.** Since the facade applies the registered schema on read, the kernel loads the credentials before the configuration (the boot order). A stored configuration that names a missing credential would be `invalid_output` in `configuration_activation`; this does not happen, because the migrated document comes from rows with an FK and every write goes through the refinement.
- **Protected references.** `referencedCredentialIds` returns the `secret` fields of the providers, `gitCredentialId` and `githubCredentialId`, exactly what the FKs protected; a `secret` in `tools` stays unprotected, as before.
- **`track` on load.** `ConfigStore.load()` reports the loaded configuration to `host-config`; afterwards, the `settings/v1` subscription keeps the current reference.
- **Credential adapter.** `credential_invalid` becomes `invalid` with the message, `credential_missing` becomes `missing`, `credential_name_taken` becomes `conflict`, `credential_kind_immutable` becomes `immutable` and `credential_storage_unavailable` becomes `MissingKeyError` (the class lives in `credentials/service.ts`); other errors propagate.
- **Boot.** `startBundles` accepts `embedded` besides the adapter; `bundleCount` excludes all built-in bundles and becomes 5. On shutdown, the `$disconnect` happens in `platform.stop()`, before `pool.dispose()`.
- **Dependencies.** The kernel depends on `postgres`; `@prisma/client` goes away; `@prisma/adapter-pg` stays only as a devDependency of the test fixtures, and `storage` as a devDependency. The root `.gitignore` now ignores `bundles/foundation/postgres/src/generated/prisma/`.
- **Kernel tests.** `tests/helpers/platform.ts` starts the installed core from `dist/bundles` with the built-in foundation `postgres-test` (an ID different from the installed `postgres`, so as not to produce `duplicate_id`). K-1 compares the whole read as a string and the response of `replace` as a string with `updatedAt` replaced, because the clock differs between the two writes. The cipher tests of `credentials.test.ts` become adapter tests on PostgreSQL (`v1:` envelope, a new nonce on each write, a blank key equal to none, a malformed key refused at load).

### Cross-reviews and notes for the next stages

- **Registrations per generation (X-10).** `storage/v1.defineType`, `settings/v1.register`/`subscribe` and `events/v1.register` become keyed by `(id, geração da chamada)` in stage 4 (D4-11): the same owner registers the same ID once per generation, and disposing of generation N does not remove the registration of N+1. In a single process every key has generation 0, which is the behavior of this stage.
- **Owner of the configuration (X-6).** `host-config` stays in the kernel and, in stage 6, gains the contract `host-config/v1` (`current`, `replace`), with the reference in `runtime/contracts`; the core receives no product configuration. The split of the space `host-config.configuration/v1` among the owners (providers, `direct`, `git`, tools) moves from stage 6/7 to stage 10.
- **Stage 4.** Local-only methods (`settings/v1.get`/`set`/`entry` with a definition object, `secrets/v1.view` and `registerReferences`, `postgres.database/v1.client`) do not cross the envelope: the definitions become IDs registered over RPC, and the kernel-only methods stay in the kernel process, where the foundation and the core run.
- **Stage 5.** `runtime` adopts `runtime.thread.events/<id>`, follows the feed and adds `NOTIFY`; it uses `secrets/v1.redactions()` instead of `view()`, because it runs in the child process; it takes over the disk policy; and it stops using `postgres.database/v1` for Thread and Project.
- **Stages 6/7.** Offline export and import in NDJSON; the import increments `storageGeneration`.

### Open points

- From the "Stage 2" item of stage 1: the output of `events/v1.append` is in S-10; the rule of the backends in `providers` (D-20) follows from the existing check that the core requires a contract that no active foundation provides (test A-4); `hostId` is in S-5.
- Still open: the tool settings values remain in the `tools` section of the configuration and do not yet reach the tools through `settings/v1` (D-9 holds until that switch), and the persisted bundle generation counter (S-5).

## Stage 4 — tool validation (4-WP4-C)

- `BoundTool.check(payload)` is synchronous (`input.safeParse` + the existing issue mapping) and `toTool` propagates it; `Tool.check?` is optional and may return a promise (remote tools). The `ToolPayloadCheck` of the `tool` package is structurally equal to that of `bundle/contracts` and uses `ToolIssue`.
- `ToolStorage.validate` returns `ToolCall | Promise<ToolCall>`: it is asynchronous only when the tool's `check` returns a promise. Tools without `check` or with a synchronous `check` stay on the synchronous path; their `execute` keeps validating twice as today, and that of asynchronous tools validates once and executes with `check.data`. Asynchronous failures produce the same `invalid_payload` (message `Tool call payload failed validation: <nome>`, issues from `check`).
- In `agent`, `validatedCalls` is asynchronous: all calls are parsed first (as today) and validated in order, stopping at the first failure, inside the same `try`; events, messages and the repair counter do not change.

## Stage 4 — runtime seams (4-WP4-A2)

- `callGeneration`/`generationOf` are exported by `bundle/contracts` (an additive change in `contracts/index.ts`). Every call created by the runtime gets the non-enumerable property; without a parent call, `generation ?? defaultGeneration ?? 0` holds, and `dispatch` passes `runtime.operationGeneration ?? 0`.
- `LayeredDirectory.register` writes to the base layer: kernel bundles register through `activeView` and their providers must be in `kernelDirectory`. `active` returns the base's provider before the overlay; `setOverlay` emits `removed` for the records of the old overlay and `added` for the new ones to each subscriber.
- The marker `x-doric-local` is now placed in the position of each local value that has a codec: `disposable` for the basic `disposableSchema` (by identity) and `callback`/`object` when the author declares `.meta({ 'x-doric-local': '<tipo>' })` in the schema. If some local value of a member has no codec, the whole member stays `{ "x-doric-local": true }`. `functionSchema()` still has no declared codec, so the inputs of hooks and tools stay `true`; the `disposableSchema` outputs become `disposable`.
- Generated tuples carry `minItems` (leading items that are not optional in the member's direction, so an item with `default` is optional in the input and required in the output) and `maxItems` when they have no rest. The `contracts.published.json` baselines of `core` and `postgres` were regenerated by the build's `baseline` command. The other baselines did not change.
- `contractDocument`, `RecordedContract`, `ContractDocument`, `JSON_SCHEMA_DIALECT` and `schemaPath` were moved to `runtime/json-schema.ts`. `build/schemas.ts` re-exports them.
- Options of `activateEnvironment`: `preActive` joins the set of active bundles of the dependency check. `hooksProvider: 'remote'` defines `AppHooksV1` before the loop, and each bundle of the loop is observed. `settle` runs after `activate` (the rejection gets the code the same refusal would have in process: a registration refusal becomes `registration_failed`/`cardinality_mismatch`, any other error becomes `activate_failed`) and again after `afterActivate` (`registration_failed`). `isRegistrationConflict` is exported by `runtime`.

## Stage 4 — generation-aware core (4-WP4-D)

- The partition follows D4-11 with `g = generationOf(call)`; the helper `bundles/foundation/core/src/host/partition.ts` holds the rule: a read sees the kernel's partition and generation `g` (or the active one, read from `doric.catalog/v1.get(call)`, when `g === 0`); a registration with `g === 0` collides with every live entry, and one with `g > 0` collides with the kernel and with its own generation. The catalog is read only when there is an entry of a generation other than 0, so the single-process mode does no new read.
- `RegisteredTool`/`RegisteredSkill` returned by `list` do not gain the `generation` field: it stays in an internal entry, so that the public shape (and `GET /tools`) does not change.
- `tools/v1.list` now accepts `call?: CallArg` in its type (`contracts/basic/tools.ts`, a type-only change, with no effect on the published schema), so that `skills/v1.register` resolves `allowed-tools` with `list(call)` in the generation of the registrant.
- `hooks/v1`: the definition of a bundle contract is kept per `(id, g)`; `doric.*` contracts have a single state. The handlers are in a list per ID with the generation of whoever registered, and `execute` filters by the call's view before sorting by `catalog.order` and `seq`. `whenDefined` observes the definition of the caller's own generation; a kernel bundle (`g === 0`) observes only kernel definitions, which is consistent with the residence rule (the kernel does not call generation contracts during `activate`).
- X-10 facades (`storage/v1.defineType`, `settings/v1.register`/`subscribe`, `events/v1.register`): entries per `(id, g)`. In `settings/v1`, the subscription belongs to the entry of its generation (the `dispose` of one generation never removes another's), but a write notifies the subscribers of all live entries of the same ID, because a single `core.settings/v1` document holds the space for all generations.
- The view tests call the methods of the core's services directly, with calls pinned by `createHopCall({ generation })` and a fake `Services` (catalog, diagnostics and in-memory backend), because the generation runner (WP4-B) does not exist yet. The end-to-end evidence is left to WP4-E.

## Stage 4 — envelope (4-WP4-A1)

- The envelope's code lives in `packages/bundle/src/runtime/envelope/` and is not re-exported by `runtime/index.ts` (a file of A2): the process (4-WP4-B) imports it through the package's relative path. The browser-safety test covers `runtime/envelope/index.js` (only `zod`).
- Codec table per `(contrato, método)` in `envelope/values.ts`, not per schema identity in `contracts/basic/common.ts`: `functionSchema()` creates a new schema on each use. Basic and platform methods without an entry in the table follow a fixed rule: with no local value, they cross; serializable input and `disposableSchema` output, optimistic registration; any other local value, `local_only`. Bundle contracts use the `x-doric-local` markers of the schemas (`true` refuses the method). A test enumerates all basic and platform methods.
- Refused with `local_only` in stage 4: `tools/v1.list`, `secrets/v1.registerReferences` and `view`, `models/v1.resolve`, `models.provider/v1.create`, `commands/v1.register`, `ui/v1.*` and `navigation/v1.register`/`subscribe` (synchronous callbacks of the client, stage 8).
- Callbacks cross in three modes: `async` (hook handlers and callbacks of bundle contracts; returns the promise of the `invoke`), `listener` (`settings/v1.subscribe`; fires without waiting, a failure goes to diagnostics as `listener_failed`) and `scoped` (`hooks/v1.whenDefined`). In `scoped`, the kernel's proxy is synchronous: it opens an execution with its own handle and returns a cleanup that releases that handle. The generation runs the callback in a child scope of the principal's current scope; on release, it disposes of that scope (dropping the registrations made in the callback) and then calls the callback's cleanup.
- Exactness at the edges: the side that has the real definition (the sender) sends the result of `safeParse` when it passes and the original value when it fails, so that the refusal and the message stay those of the core. On the way back, it parses again when it passes. This holds for hook inputs (`execute` and the registered handler), `transform` outputs, values of `settings/v1` (`get`, `entry`, `set`, `subscribe` inputs) and payloads of `events/v1.append`.
- Mirrors in the kernel: known `doric.*` hooks contracts become the real object; the others are `defineHooks` with JSON Schema validators, cached by shape in the link. Events and storage types become `z.looseObject({})` with the validator; settings become only the validator. Risk: the core's introspection of the schema of a storage type (index fields, `fingerprintOf`) sees the mirror, so an index with a nonexistent field defined in the generation is not refused in the kernel. No generation bundle defines storage types today.
- Generation invariant in the kernel: a `call`/`invoke` that arrives over the link of generation N must carry N or a generation the kernel itself already sent over that link (for example 0, when a callback called from an unpinned kernel call calls the kernel back). Any other gets `protocol_error`.
- A `cancel` that arrives after the call's deadline aborts the provider's signal with `TimeoutError`, as in process, because the sender's signal aborted on the deadline.
- Optimistic registrations: `ProcessLink.activating(bundleId)` marks the activation. While it lasts, the refusals are kept for `settle(bundleId)`; outside it, they become `registration_failed` in the process's diagnostics. The handles exported for the operation (tool, callback) are released when the registration is disposed of or refused.
- When the link closes, the `onClose` listeners run before the rejection of the pending calls (`generation_lost`, details `{ generation, incarnation }`). After that, the side that remains disposes of everything the other side registered in it. `registerRemote` removes the remote record from the directory when the link closes.
- `object` objects: the imported handle is a proxy that only answers to the handle itself (`then` and `dispose` read `undefined`, so as not to be taken for a thenable or a Disposable). Any other read throws `local_only`, and on returning to the process of origin the handle becomes the original object again.
- `ControlMessage.init` is typed as `unknown`, and the one that validates it is the runner (4-WP4-B), owner of `GenerationInit`.

## Stage 4 — Node process layer (4-WP4-B)

- Residence (D4-1, revised by X-8): `process/partition.ts` puts `foundation`, `core` and every built-in bundle in the kernel; installed `others` go to the generation. The published order is the kernel's followed by the generation's (D4-2). The kernel activates its partition with `preActive` = the generation's scheduled bundles, so the dependency of a kernel bundle on a generation provider is satisfied by the catalog. `kernel_requires_generation` is not emitted: with the revision, it would only apply when no enabled bundle provides the contract, a case the resolver already handles with its own codes.
- Pending (G-11): the wait of the kernel's stubs for an active generation (`onActive`) was not implemented; with no active generation, `dispatch` answers `unavailable` as today. There is no consumer in the kernel in stage 4.
- The generation counter is per kernel (boot = 1, each accepted `publish` = +1), not the process-global counter of `startHost`. A refused `publish` (`kernel_restart_required`) does not use up a number.
- `kernel_restart_required` compares a signature of the kernel's partition: ID, version, phase, state and origin of the kernel's entries, the kernel's providers per contract and the recovery reason.
- `doric.generation/v1` (minimal API, `describe()`/`hold(execution)`) is a platform contract in `contracts/platform.ts`, provided by the kernel and announced to the generation as a remote provider (with no new control messages). `describe` and `hold` answer for the operation's generation (`generationOf(call)`, or the active one when 0). The manager's `acquire` and `hold` use the same count (`holds` per generation, `ExecutionRef | { window }`). Draining stops when there are no holds; pending calls do not enter the criterion (the `ProcessLink` does not expose them), and every running execution already has a hold.
- `doric.catalog/v1`, `doric.diagnostics/v1` and `doric.bundle/v1` are local in the generation's process. The catalog comes from `init` and is replaced by the `catalog` messages: the kernel sends each live process the snapshot of that process's own generation whenever a generation becomes active. The local diagnostics join the process's store with the catalog's states for the kernel bundles; `report` writes to the local store and the process sends the snapshot (`diagnostics`) to the kernel, which merges it into the view of that generation. The diagnostics of a generation in the kernel have the kernel's bundles, those of the generation from the last snapshot received, and the errors of both in time order.
- The runner registers the platform's local providers directly in the internal directory; `AnnouncingDirectory` announces (`provide`/`unprovide`) only local records that are not `doric.*`. The kernel's providers arrive in `init.kernelProviders`, except the three local ones above.
- Activation barrier: `activateEnvironment` gained the option `onPrincipal(entry, principal)` (a file of A2, an additive change), called right after the principal is created. The runner uses it to open `link.activating(id)` (closed when the next bundle starts or when the loop ends) and so that `scoped` callbacks find the principal already during `activate`. A test confirms that the refusal of a duplicate tool in the generation quarantines the bundle with the same reason as in the single-process mode.
- The Node runner rereads the bundle's manifest (`readInstalledBundle`) in `afterActivate` to get the tools and skills of v0 manifests, instead of carrying them in `init`.
- Start failure: after `startAttempts` processes that exit before `ready`, the generation becomes `failed`, its bundles appear `quarantined` with `generation_failed` (`Generation N could not start: its process exited before it was ready (code X, signal Y; ...).`), the kernel logs `Bundle quarantined` for each one and activates an empty view (only the kernel's partition), firing `onActive(N)` so that `acquire` resolves. In `publish`, the failure leaves the active generation as it is and returns `failed`.
- Crash of a ready generation: the `onLost` listeners run inside the link's closing, before the `generation_lost` rejections; the holds of the lost incarnation are discarded (the later `release` does nothing). An active generation is recreated with `incarnation + 1` and, when it becomes ready, fires `onActive` and `onGenerationReadyHook`; a draining generation just stops. The kernel records `generation_lost` in diagnostics (and `generation_failed` when it gives up).
- In recovery mode the kernel starts no generations (as today, the remaining bundles stay inactive); `acquire` then waits, abortable by the signal.
- `ajv@8.20.0` (already in the lockfile) became a direct dependency of `bundle` (`package.json` and the `packages/bundle` entry of `package-lock.json`); the implementation is `process/ajv.ts`, with a cache by canonical JSON.
- The table of `tests/values.test.ts` (A1) gained `doric.generation/v1` (`describe` crosses; `hold` is optimistic with `Disposable`).

## Stage 4 — host integration (4-WP4-E)

- `startBundles` now uses `startKernel` (with `errorClasses: [ToolErrorObject]`) and returns `catalog()` (tools and skills of the active generation) and `acquire(execution, signal)`, which pins the prompt to the active generation and returns a `BundleLease` (`call`, `tools(call)`, `skills(call)`, `release`). The tools are read on each call of `ToolsV1.list`; the skills, which are asynchronous, are cached per generation and reread in `onActive`, because `GET /tools` and `requireToolConfigs` need a synchronous read.
- The configuration's `Generation` lost `catalog`. `ConfigService` gained `catalog()`, and `GET /tools` and the validation of `replace` read the active generation through it. The Direct executor acquires the lease before `attach` and releases it after the adapter's `release`; the prompt's tools and skills come from the lease.
- Suppression of generation loss: a `tool.failed` whose error is `handler_failed` with cause `generation_lost`, with the signal already aborted, is not written. The executor writes the history at that moment and does not write it again in `finally`, because the agent's `release()` would answer the call with "Tool call did not complete…". So the history stays as in a host crash: the call without a result, which the resumed run answers with the interrupted-call result (Q10). The design assumed that `finally` would already store the pending call; it does not.
- `pauseForGenerationLoss` is synchronous and does not take the project lock: `onLost` runs before the rejection of the pending calls, and waiting for the lock would let the rejection arrive with the signal not yet aborted. `held` is also checked in the `run` loop, not only in `start()`, so that the same loop does not take the next prompt of the queue after the pause.
- Exhaustion in `resumeHeld`: it uses `ThreadStore.failPrompt` (the same events, result and result delegated to the parent that the boot writes), then publishes the new events of the Thread and of the parent and puts in the parent's queue, in memory, the entry that `failPrompt` accepted for it.
- The wiring of `onLost`/`onActive` lives in `recoverGenerationLoss` (`bundles/boot.ts`), used by `index.ts` and by the tests. When the lost generation was not the active one (it was draining), the resumption starts at once, without waiting for `onActive`. The recovery operations (`suspendForGenerationLoss`, `resumeAfterGenerationLoss`) are exposed by the `GenerationRecovery` type of `workspace/service.ts`, without changing `WorkspaceService` in `types.ts`.
- E4 parity: the test "a Host control failure fails the tool call with that same error as its cause" now compares class, name and message of the cause, not the identity of the object. The tool runs in the generation, so the cause crosses two process boundaries and arrives reconstructed. What is observable (the `ToolErrorObject` `handler_failed` with the cause of the Host's refusal) does not change.
- Fixtures: `tests/fixtures/generation-bundles/{probe,crash-on-activate}` are plain JS (v1 manifest, `contracts.json`, `package.json` with `type: module`). The tests copy `dist/bundles` and the fixtures to temporary roots in `agents/doric/dist-tests`, so that `bundle`, `tool` and `zod` resolve through the repository's `node_modules`, as in the staged bundles. The gate of the `probe` tool uses files with an absolute path (`<gate>.started` with the pid, `<gate>.release`).

## Stage 4 — design decisions (D4-1 to D4-18)

The 4-WP4-\* sections above detail the implementation; this list records the decisions of the stage's design and where the implementation adjusted them.

- **D4-1 Residence.** Foundation, core and built-in bundles stay in the kernel; installed `others` go to the generation's process. Adjustment (X-8, 4-WP4-B): `kernel_requires_generation` is never thrown; the kernel activates with `preActive` = the generation's bundles, and a missing provider stays with the resolver.
- **D4-2 Published order.** The kernel's order followed by the generation's order.
- **D4-3 Respawn.** Keeps the generation number; `incarnation` counts the processes.
- **D4-4 Generation of the operation.** Inherited along the call chain (`generationOf`, `callGeneration`, the envelope's `generation` field); 0 = kernel or unpinned.
- **D4-5 Envelope.** Adds `generation`, `provider`, `oneway`, `invoke` and `release`; reserved stream types; a separate control plane.
- **D4-6 Call semantics.** `cancel` is advisory (no local rejection); `invoke` is not a hop; the kernel overwrites `caller.generation` from the link.
- **D4-7 Errors.** A codec with the order of own properties, registered classes, synthetic prototypes by name and copied `stack`; `code: 'internal'` is only metadata.
- **D4-8 Local values.** They cross only in basic and platform contracts, through a fixed codec table; local methods defined by bundles are `local_only`.
- **D4-9 Synchronous registrations.** Optimistic, with an activation barrier (`settle`) that preserves the quarantine codes.
- **D4-10 Schemas.** JSON Schema validation with ajv 2020 behind `SchemaValidators`; tuples with `minItems`/`maxItems`; regenerated baselines.
- **D4-11 Core per generation.** Tools, skills and hooks partitioned by the generation of the operation; catalog and diagnostics reads receive the call.
- **D4-12 Switch.** Activates N+1, switches, fires `onGenerationReadyHook`, drains N through the leases without a deadline; a change in the kernel requires a restart (`kernel_restart_required`). Draining waits for zero holds and no call in flight (`ProcessLink.inFlight`: pending outputs + unanswered inputs); `onIdle` re-evaluates the stop.
- **D4-13 Trigger.** No external generation trigger in stage 4; `publish()` is internal to the kernel.
- **D4-14 Crash.** `onLost` before the rejections; only the active generation is recreated; 3 start attempts; after that, quarantine `generation_failed` and an empty view.
- **D4-15 Tool execution.** Each tool runs in its bundle's process; the kernel uses `RemoteTool` with an exact asynchronous `check`; `tool`/`agent` accept asynchronous validation.
- **D4-16 Loss with pinned prompts.** The prompts pause with `host_restarted`, the Threads are held and resume with the budget and the order of the boot. Adjustment (4-WP4-E): immediate resumption when the lost generation was draining.
- **D4-17 Desktop.** Reuses `runGeneration`/`ProcessLink` with `hooksProvider: 'local'`; the main process plays the role of the kernel with platform providers only (stage 8).
- **D4-18 Prisma.** No schema change or SQL migration in stage 4.
- **E2E gate.** The image `docker build -f agents/doric/.Dockerfile --target docker-runtime` starts without a change to the `.Dockerfile` (the kernel stays PID 1 and receives the SIGTERM alone). Three harness runs (21/21 API, 18/18 UI on the first): the UI is identical to the baseline; in the API only the `stack` difference in `17-failures-and-http` remains, which already existed in stages 1 and 2/3. In one of the three runs, `14-delegation` showed the race between the end of the parent's prompt and the child's result (the result arrived with the parent still busy: `prompt.accepted{queued: true}` and `prompt.queued`, and therefore `lastSequence` + 1 in `15` and `18`). Both outcomes are current behavior; the IPC latency (R8) makes the second possible, and nothing was changed to hide it.

## Stages 5 to 7 — contract amendments (57-WP-C)

- **`models/v1` and `models.provider/v1` refined (57 §2.10).** The provider has asynchronous `kind`, `advertised` and `catalog` and `create({ providerId, values, secret? })`; `models/v1` gains `kinds`, `kind`, `catalog` and `prepare`. `ModelEntry`/`listModels` go away and give way to `CatalogModel`/`catalog` (`{ status: 'read', models } | { status: 'absent' | 'failed' }`). `prepare` receives the configuration as a loose record, because `packages/bundle` does not depend on `DoricConfig`.
- **Crossing by marker in the basic contracts.** A basic method without a fixed entry in the envelope crosses through the `x-doric-local` markers of its schemas (the same rule as the bundle contracts); without a codec it is still refused. The `LanguageModel` is marked `object` (it returns to the process that created it as the original object; in another process any use is `local_only`) and the `secret` of `create` is marked `callback`, read before each request.
- **`toLanguageModel`.** The providers of `llms` are object literals, which the envelope would copy; the provider returns `toLanguageModel(provider)`, which copies the own descriptors (getters included) to an object with its own prototype. An object that is already not a literal comes back unchanged.
- **`tools/v1.list` crosses.** The tools go out as `tool` handles; in the process of origin they come back as the registered tool and in the others as `RemoteTool`. The type of `list` stays synchronous for the callers in the core process; a remote caller receives a promise of the same list and does `await`.
- **Scope.** The storage and secrets amendments (A2–A9) already came from 23-WP-A; `host-config/v1` stays with 57-WP-0. No change in `bundles/foundation/core` was needed in this WP.

## Stages 5 to 7 — `runtime` contracts and adapter capture (57-WP-0)

- **Contracts in `runtime/contracts` (57 §2, §3.2).** `runtime/v1`, `runtime.threads/v1` (the stage 1 controls plus the operations of `WorkspaceService.threads`), `runtime.workspace/v1`, `runtime.lease/v1` (the four stage 1 methods plus the lease-dependent reads), `runtime.terminals/v1`, `runtime.hooks/v1`, `executor/v1`, `sandbox/v1`, `host-config/v1`, the event `runtime.prompt-finished/v1` and the storage types `runtime.project/v1`, `runtime.thread/v1`, `runtime.history/v1`, `runtime.migration/v1`. The domain types were copied from `agents/doric/src/lib/workspace` without change; the kernel's copy goes away in 57-WP-K. Minimal API.
- **`RuntimeThreadControls`.** The seven stage 1 controls sit in an interface of their own, extended by `RuntimeThreads`, so that the parity test with `packages/host` stays exact. The partial providers of stage 1 (the kernel adapter and the test fakes of `files`, `git` and `threads`) now use `defineService(ref.id, …)`, with no change in behavior, until the `runtime` provides the whole contract.
- **Structural `host-config/v1`.** `ConfigurationValue`/`DoricConfiguration` mirror `ConfigInput`/`DoricConfig` without depending on the kinds catalog of `llms`; `replace` receives the body as it arrived (`unknown`) and the provider validates it, returning `invalid` with `'The Doric configuration is invalid.'` for a body that the schema refuses (today 422 `invalid_config`).
- **Local markers.** `runtime/v1.watch` uses `callback` and `disposable`; `executor/v1` marks the `PromptSession` and the `HistoryReader` as `object`; `sandbox/v1.acquire` marks the lease and the `AbortSignal` as `object`. `describe`, `discard`, `machines` and `machine` of `sandbox/v1` are asynchronous and serializable. `executor/v1.formats` stays synchronous, as in the design, because `runtime` and `direct` are in the same process.
- **Store interfaces.** `bundles/runtime/runtime/src/host/store/types.ts` freezes `ProjectStore`/`ThreadStore` without `messages` and `checkpoints`: `history`, `saveHistory` (with `updatedAt`) and `saveCheckpoint` (without `updatedAt`) replace `saveMessages`/`saveCheckpoint`, and `rewind` applies the executor's patch in the same commit.
- **Manifest without the bundle build step.** `bundles/runtime/runtime/manifest.json` exists, but the build is still only `tsc` and there is no `contracts-baseline`: the build CLI refuses a manifest whose `provides` no host part registers. 57-WP-R2, owner of `src/host/index.ts`, adds the `cli.js build` step and the baseline.
